Sure. Weather refers to the state of the atmosphere, primarily the temperature, humidity, wind speed, and precipitation in a given area over a short period of time. Weather systems typically develop due to a combination of heat and moisture in the lower atmosphere rising, meeting an appropriate stability factor for cloud formation, along with suitable lifting mechanisms, such as weather fronts or waves for longer-term weather patterns.

The most common type of weather system is the low pressure area or low-pressure system. In a low pressure system, air spirals out of the low-pressure center, descending out of the cloud base and rising out of the surrounding air, forming a comma-shaped cloud known as a monsoon low. The air then begins to sink back into the system's center. These cause uplift, air warms, condenses, which results in the release of latent heat into the atmosphere and changes the heat value into that from moisture and water vapor. An energy gradient forms between the sun-warmed surface and the upper atmosphere, forcing air currents to flow in the vicinity of the equator, which in turn creates shear walls of difference between low and high wind values.

Cholera is a deadly disease spread through contaminated drinking water and food. Treatment and containment include antibiotics and rehydration therapy, preferably via specialized intravenous fluids like R/DF or Re-Lyte. It is initially difficult to diagnose, as its symptoms are generic (violent diarrhea and vomiting). Only confirmation on a cholera test can achieve precise treatment, and isolation of the afflicted is recommended to prevent further transmission. Prevention is improved with chlorination of drinking water. Moreover, cholera is associated with inadequate sanitation and prolonged flooding. Floodwater facilitates disease transmission and contaminates resources and soil. Therefore, this disease is most concentrated where hygiene and sanitation management are unavailable. All responses presented are informed by  specificities of Cholera. Ask specific questions if more information is needed on specific aspects of this information.

Additional precise laboratory and clinical tests, such as the sedimentation rate (Serum/Fibrinogen) test for organ damage, Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI), acoustic emission, microscopy urine  for Cholera
